We can assume w.l.o.g. that
C_{0},C_{1}\geq 1
, so that
K,L\geq 1
.

A first case.
We need to treat separately the case where
KN^{-2/3}>\delta_{0}
, in which case the decomposition (
2.38
) is not meaningful.
In that case, we have
N\leq(K/\delta_{0})^{3/2}\leq Cq^{2a}L^{6a}
so that

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}([1,\infty))^{q}}}\right]\leq N^{q}\leq C^{q}q^{2aq}L^{6aq}\leq C^{q}q^{2aq}B_{\max}^{2q},

using that
L\leq CB_{\max}^{1/(1+3a)}\leq CB_{\max}^{1/3a}
because
B_{\max}\geq 1
.

Therefore, we can now restrict ourselves to the case
KN^{-2/3}\leq\delta_{0}
and successively deal with the intervals in the decomposition (
2.38
).

First interval:
(1,1+KN^{-2/3}]
.
We define
y=KN^{-2/3}
and
z=1+y+\mathrm{i}y
, thus by (
2.36
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((1,1+KN^{-2/3}]}}\right)^{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(2yN)^{q}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im s_{N}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CyN)^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right).

Note that
z\in\mathcal{T}
(recall
y\leq\delta_{0}
because we ruled out the opposite case before), so we can apply Proposition
2.7
.
(i)
to the first term.
On the other hand, by (
B.15
) (and continuity to apply it on the boundary of the trapezoid region), we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq Cy/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert b(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C\sqrt{y}
.
Thus, we obtain, using in the second inequality
y\leq\delta_{0}
and
y=(qL^{3})^{4a/3}N^{-2/3}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((1,1+KN^{-2/3}]}}\right)^{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(Cq)^{q/2}+(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}y^{q/2}+(CN)^{q}y^{3q/2}

\displaystyle\leq(Cq)^{q/2}+(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}+C^{q}(qL^{3})^{2aq}\leq(Cq^{2a}B_{\max}^{2})^{q},

using
a\geq 1
,
B_{\max}\geq 1
and
L\leq CB_{\max}^{1/(1+3a)}\leq CB_{\max}^{1/3a}
.

Second interval:
I\coloneqq(1+KN^{-2/3},1+\delta_{0}]
.
We split this interval into several smaller intervals.
Let
a_{0}\coloneqq K
and
a_{j+1}\coloneqq a_{j}+a_{j}^{1/4}/L
for
j\geq 0
.
We define

\kappa_{j}\coloneqq a_{j}N^{-2/3},\qquad x_{j}\coloneqq 1+\kappa_{j},\qquad y_{j}\coloneqq\dfrac{a_{j}^{1/4}}{L}N^{-2/3},\qquad q_{j}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lfloor\frac{a_{j}^{3/(4a)}}{L^{3}}}}\right\rfloor,

and consider the intervals
I_{j}\coloneqq(x_{j},x_{j}+y_{j}]
. Note that
x_{0}=1+KN^{-2/3}
and
x_{j+1}=x_{j}+y_{j}
so that
I=[1+KN^{-2/3},1+\delta_{0}]\subset\bigcup_{j=0}^{j_{\mathrm{\max}}}I_{j}
with
j_{\max}\coloneqq\max\{j\geq 0,\kappa_{j}\leq\delta_{0}\}
.
Using Minkowski’s inequality first and then that
q_{j}\geq q_{0}=q
(together with the fact that
\mathcal{N}(I_{j})
is integer valued), we have

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}(I)^{q}}}\right]^{1/q}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{j_{\max}}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}(I_{j})^{q}}}\right]^{1/q}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{j_{\max}}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}(I_{j})^{q_{j}}}}\right]^{1/q}.

(2.39)

Now, in order to apply (
2.37
) with
(x,y)=(x_{j},y_{j})
for
j\leq j_{\max}
, we need to check that the conditions are satisfied. We have
x_{j}+\mathrm{i}y_{j}\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{T}
because
y_{j}\leq\kappa_{j}
(using here
a_{j}\geq a_{0}=K\geq 1
and
L\geq 1
) and
\kappa_{j}\leq\delta_{0}
by definition of
j_{\max}
.
The condition
y_{j}\leq\kappa_{j}^{1/4}/(C_{0}\sqrt{N})
holds because
L\geq C_{0}
. Finally,
y_{j}\geq\sqrt{C_{1}q_{j}}/(N\sqrt{\kappa_{j}})
is implied by
a_{j}^{3/4-3/(8a)}L^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{C_{1}}
which holds because
a\geq 1
,
a_{j}\geq 1
and
L\geq C_{1}
.
Therefore, (
2.37
) yields

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}(I_{j})^{q_{j}}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{C_{2}a_{j}^{3/(4a)-3/4}}{L^{4}}}}\right)^{q_{j}/2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{C_{2}a_{j}^{3/(4a)-3/4}}{L^{2}}}}\right)^{q_{j}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{C_{2}B_{\max}}{L^{3a+1}}}}\right)^{q_{j}}\leq 3e^{-q_{j}},

(2.40)

using
a_{j}^{3/(4a)-3/4}\leq 1
and the definition of
L
.
Combining this with (
2.39
) and using
q_{j}\geq a_{j}^{3/4a}/L^{3}-1
,
a_{j}\geq K+jK^{1/4}/L
and then
K=(L^{3}q)^{4a/3}
together with a sum-integral comparison, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}(I)^{q}}}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-q_{j}/q}\leq 3\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{(K+jK^{1/4}/L)^{3/4a}}{qL^{3}}+\frac{1}{q}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3e^{1/q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-(1+x/(LK^{3/4}))^{3/4a}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x}}\right)=3e^{1/q}LK^{3/4}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-(1+y)^{3/4a}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq Caq^{a}L^{1+3a}\leq(Cq)^{a}B_{\max},

where we used in the last inequality that
L\leq CB_{\max}^{1/(1+3a)}
.

Third interval:
(1+\delta_{0},\infty)
.
Here, we simply use the fact that the probability of having an eigenvalue larger than
1+\delta_{0}
is exponentially small, see Lemma
5.1
.
(ii)
.
This yields

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathcal{N}((1+\delta_{0},\infty))^{q}}}\right]\leq N^{q}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exists k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\delta_{0}}}\right)\leq CN^{q}e^{-cN}\leq(Cq)^{q},

using here that
e^{cN}\geq(cN)^{q}/q!\geq N^{q}/(Cq)^{q}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.7
.
(ii)
.

Let
z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{T}
. By symmetry, we can assume that
x\geq 0
, which necessarily means that
x>1+y
.
We define
z_{0}=1+y+\mathrm{i}y
.
Following the same argument as in
[
19
, Proposition 3.5]
(this uses (
B.16
)), we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C\bigl(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert+\re\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s_{N}(z_{0})-s_{N}(z)}}\right)\\
+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{0})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{0})}}\right\rvert\bigr),

as well as
\re(s_{N}(z_{0})-s_{N}(z))\leq\frac{2}{Ny}\mathcal{N}((1,\infty))
.
We thus obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq C^{q}\Biggl(\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\mathcal{N}((1,\infty))^{q}]}{(Ny)^{q}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{0})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\Biggr).

(2.41)

For the first term on the RHS of (
2.41
), we use Lemma
2.9
to obtain

\mathbb{E}[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}]\leq\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{(Ny)^{q}},

(2.42)

by distinguishing the two cases in Lemma
2.9
as follows.
For the first case,
y\geq\sqrt{C^{\prime}q}/(N\sqrt{\kappa})
, we have, using that
\kappa\geq y
for
z\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle\frac{(Cq)^{q/2}}{N^{q}(y\kappa)^{q/2}}+\frac{(Cq)^{q}}{(Ny)^{2q}\kappa^{q/2}}+\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{N}(z))^{q}}{N^{q}\kappa^{q/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(Cq)^{q/2}}{(Ny)^{q}}+\dfrac{C^{q}q^{q}}{(Ny)^{2q}\kappa^{q/2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Ny\sqrt{\kappa}}{(C^{\prime}q)^{1/2}}}}\right)^{q/2}+\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{N^{q}y^{q/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(Cq)^{q/2}}{(Ny)^{q}}+\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{N^{q}y^{q/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{(Ny)^{q}},

using
y\leq\delta_{0}
and
a,B_{\max}\geq 1
in the last inequality.
In the second case,
y\leq\sqrt{C^{\prime}q}/(N\sqrt{\kappa})
, we can bound the second term of the bound as follows

\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{N}(z))^{q/2}}{N^{q/2}}\leq\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q/2}}{N^{q/2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{(C^{\prime}q)^{1/2}}{Ny\sqrt{\kappa}}}}\right)^{q/2}=\frac{(Cq^{a/2+1/4}B_{\max}^{1/2})^{q}}{N^{q}y^{q/2}\kappa^{q/4}}\leq\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{(Ny)^{q}}.

Then, using Lemma
2.10
for the second term on the RHS of (
2.41
) and Proposition
2.7
.
(i)
for the third term (note that
z_{0}\in\mathcal{T}
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{\max})^{q}}{(Ny)^{q}}+\frac{(Cq)^{2aq}B_{\max}^{2q}}{(Ny)^{q}}+\frac{(Cq)^{q/2}}{(Ny)^{q}}+\frac{(Cq^{a}B_{N}(z_{0}))^{q}}{(N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z_{0}}}\right\rvert\lvert z_{0}^{2}-1\rvert^{1/2})^{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(Cq)^{2aq}B_{\max}^{2q}}{(Ny)^{q}},

using in particular that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z_{0}}}\right\rvert\lvert z_{0}^{2}-1\rvert^{1/2}\geq cy
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

3
Applications of the Helffer–Sjöstrand formula

In this section, we show how the
Helffer–Sjöstrand
formula can be used to deduce, from a local law, bounds on linear statistics and on the anisotropy introduced below.
Let
f\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a test function, with sufficient integrability and regularity conditions so that the following quantities make sense.
Recall our notation for linear statistics:

L_{N}(f)\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N}f(\lambda_{k})-N\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)=N\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{N}-\mu_{V_{\alpha}})(x).

(3.1)

We also introduce the following quantity, sometimes called the
anisotropy
(see e.g.
[
9
]
),

A_{N}(f)\coloneqq N^{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{f(\lambda)-f(\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{N}-\mu_{V_{\alpha}})(\lambda)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{N}-\mu_{V_{\alpha}})(\lambda^{\prime}),

(3.2)

where we take the convention
\frac{f(\lambda)-f(\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}=f^{\prime}(\lambda)
when
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
. This quantity arises as an error term in the study of the convergence of
L_{N}(f)
towards a Gaussian law and showing that this term in indeed negligible is often one of the main obstacle. Note however, that these quantities are scaled by appropriate factors of
N
so that they are theoretically of order 1 for regular enough
f
.
In this section, we show how to use Helffer–Sjöstrand formula to control these quantities.

First recall Helffer–Sjöstrand formula, see e.g.
[
12
, Proposition C.1]
for a reference.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{n+1}(\mathbb{R})
. We define the pseudo-analytic extension of
f
of degree
n
through

\widetilde{f}_{n}(x+\mathrm{i}y)\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k!}(\mathrm{i}y)^{k}f^{(k)}(x).

Let
\chi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\chi(0)=1
.
Then, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

f(\lambda)=\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\overline{\partial}(\widetilde{f}_{n}(z)\chi(y))}{\lambda-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

where
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
in the integral and
\overline{\partial}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x}+\mathrm{i}\partial_{y})
is the antiholomorphic derivative.

There are two useful applications here.
Firstly, for a linear statistics
L_{N}(f)
with
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we take
n=1
and get

L_{N}(f)=\frac{N}{2\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)+\mathrm{i}(f(x)+\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime}(x))\chi^{\prime}(y)}}\right)(s_{N}-s_{V_{\alpha}})(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y.

(3.3)

Secondly, for the anisotropy
A_{N}(f)
with
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
, assuming that
\chi
is identically equal to 1 in a neighbourhood of 0 and applying Helffer–Sjöstrand formula with
n=2
yields, for
\lambda\neq\lambda^{\prime}
,

\frac{f(\lambda)-f(\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\overline{\partial}(\widetilde{f}_{n}(z)\chi(y))}{(\lambda-z)(\lambda^{\prime}-z)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y.

This is also true for
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
by taking the limit
\lambda^{\prime}\to\lambda
on both sides (on the RHS, domination is obtained by noting that
\lvert\overline{\partial}(\widetilde{f}_{n}(z)\chi(y))\rvert\leq Cy^{2}
for
z
in a neighbourhood of
\lambda
, because
\chi^{\prime}(y)=0
for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert
small enough).
Therefore

A_{N}(f)=\frac{N^{2}}{2\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bigg(-\frac{y^{2}}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)+\mathrm{i}\big[f(x)+\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime}(x)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\big]\chi^{\prime}(y)\bigg)(s_{N}-s_{V_{\alpha}})(z)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y.

(3.4)

As mentioned before, the following lemmas allow us to translate the local laws obtained in Section
2.2
into estimates on the moments of
L_{N}(f)
and
A_{N}(f)
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
p>2
.
For any
\delta>0
and any compact set
K_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C>0
such that the following holds.
For any
N,q\geq 1
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, if there exists
M>0
such that, for any
x\in K_{0}
and
y\in(0,\delta]
, with
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{Ny}}}\right)^{q},

(3.5)

then, for any
\eta\in(0,(\delta\wedge 1)/2]
and
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
supported in
K_{0}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{1}+\log(1/\eta)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}+\eta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}}}\right)}}\right)^{q}.

(3.6)

Remark 3.3
.

The parameter
\eta
has to be thought of as small enough so that the term involving
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
dominates.
More precisely, in the sequel, we typically want to apply this result to functions
f
where
f^{\prime}(x)
explodes at 0 like
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-3}
, therefore
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
is finite because
p>2
.
After some local
N
-dependent regularization of
f
, we can have a finite
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
but which grows polynomially fast in
N
, and therefore we choose
\eta=N^{-k}
with
k
large enough to compensate.

Having a control of
L_{N}(f)
in terms of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
is the main interest of using the local laws together with Helffer–Sjöstrand formula.
This has to be compared with other methods to obtain estimates on linear statistics, which provide bounds in terms of norms exploding in the case mentioned above: for example,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{2}
for the
a priori
bound
L_{N}(f)=O(\sqrt{N\log N})
in
[
31
, Corollary 4.16]
or
\lVert f^{(4)}\rVert_{\infty}
in the sharper bound
L_{N}(f)=O(1)
in
[
9
, Corollary 4.5]
.

Proof.

Let
\chi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathbbm{1}_{[-\delta/2,\delta/2]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-\delta,\delta]}
.
We start from (
3.3
) and rewrite the term involving
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
, by first splitting it into two parts,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}yf^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)(s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}yf^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)(s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad{}+\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\eta}yf^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)(s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

and then, for the part
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta
, we first integrate by part
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
w.r.t.
x
and then
s_{N}^{\prime}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}^{\prime}(z)
w.r.t.
y
:

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}f^{\prime\prime}(x)y\chi(y)(s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}f^{\prime}(x)y\chi(y)(s_{N}^{\prime}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}^{\prime}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}f^{\prime}(x)\partial_{y}(y\chi(y))\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}}(s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad{}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}(x)\eta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}}(s_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad{}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}(x)\eta\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}}(s_{N}(x-\mathrm{i}\eta)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(x-\mathrm{i}\eta))\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x,

where we used that
\chi(\eta)=1
because
\eta\leq\delta/2
.
Note that the two last term have the same modulus.
Therefore, we can bound
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}\leq(\frac{2}{\pi})^{q}(I_{1}^{q}+I_{2}^{q}+I_{3}^{q}+I_{4}^{q})
, where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f(x)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert yf^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\chi^{\prime}(y)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\eta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert yf^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\partial_{y}(y\chi(y))f^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2N\eta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x.

These terms are bounded using a similar method.
We detail it for
I_{3}
. Writing
I_{3}^{q}
as an integral over
x_{1},\dots,x_{q},y_{1},\dots,y_{q}
and bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\partial_{y}(y\chi(y))}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\delta}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I_{3}^{q}}}\right]\leq(CN)^{q}\idotsint_{\forall k,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y_{k}}}\right\rvert\in[\eta,\delta]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime}(x_{1})}}\right\rvert\cdots\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime}(x_{q})}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{k=1}^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{k})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{k})}}\right\rvert}}\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\cdots\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{q}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y_{1}\cdots\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y_{q}.

Then, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{k=1}^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{k})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{k})}}\right\rvert}}\right]\leq\prod_{k=1}^{q}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{k})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{k})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]^{1/q}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{N}}}\right)^{q}\prod_{k=1}^{q}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y_{k}}}\right\rvert},

(3.7)

by the assumption (
3.5
), noting that only points
z_{k}
with
x_{k}\in K_{0}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y_{k}}}\right\rvert\leq\delta
matter in the previous integral and also using
(s_{N}-s_{V_{\alpha}})(\overline{z_{k}})=\overline{(s_{N}-s_{V_{\alpha}})(z_{k})}
to apply (
3.5
) in the case
y_{k}<0
.
Therefore, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[I_{3}^{q}]\leq(CM)^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x}}\right)^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\eta}^{\delta}\frac{1}{y}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}}\right)^{q}\leq(CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}\log(1/\eta))^{q}.

Proceeding similarly, we get the bounds (for
I_{1}
note that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\chi^{\prime}(y)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\in[\delta/2,\delta]}
)

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[I_{1}^{q}]\leq(CM(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{1}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}))^{q},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[I_{2}^{q}]\leq(CM\eta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1})^{q},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[I_{4}^{q}]\leq(CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1})^{q},

which together yield (
3.6
).
∎

An analogue of the previous lemma also holds for the anisotropy term.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
p>2
.
For any
\delta>0
and any compact set
K_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C>0
such that the following holds.
For any
N,q\geq 1
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, if there exists
M>0
such that, for any
x\in K_{0}
and
y\in(0,\delta]
, with
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{2q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{Ny}}}\right)^{2q},

(3.8)

then, for any
\eta\in(0,(\delta\wedge 1)/2)
and
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
supported in
K_{0}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(CM^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{1}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}+\log(1/\eta)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}+\eta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}}}\right)}}\right)^{q}.

(3.9)

Remark 3.5
.

The parameter
\eta
has to be thought of as small enough so that the term involving
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
dominates (instead of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
in the case of
L_{N}(f)
treated in Lemma
3.2
).
However, we typically want to apply this result to functions
f
where
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
explodes at 0 like
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-3}
, instead of
f^{\prime}(x)
exploding like
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-3}
for linear statistics, see Remark
3.3
.
Similarly, a regularisation step will be required first to apply Lemma
3.4
to a function
f
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}
finite, although diverging with
N
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
3.2
:
starting from (
3.4
) and using the same integration by part tricks for the term involving
f^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)
, we get
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}\leq(\frac{2}{\pi})^{q}(J_{1}^{q}+J_{2}^{q}+J_{3}^{q}+J_{4}^{q})
, where

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N^{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f(x)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert yf^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert+y^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\chi^{\prime}(y)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N^{2}\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\eta}y^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq N^{2}\iint_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\eta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\partial_{y}(y^{2}\chi(y))f^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2N^{2}\eta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x.

Then, the same method as before yields the bounds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[J_{1}^{q}]

\displaystyle\leq(CM^{2}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{1}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}))^{q},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[J_{2}^{q}]\leq(CM^{2}\eta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1})^{q},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[J_{3}^{q}]

\displaystyle\leq(CM^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1}\log(1/\eta))^{q},\hskip 64.01869pt\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[J_{4}^{q}]\leq(CM^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{1})^{q},

which concludes the proof.
∎

When performing the master operator approach and the local law machinery to improve the bounds on the linear statistics,
one of the intermediary versions of the local law contains a bound which is not of the form of the assumption (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.2
, because it contains an additional term proportional to
1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}
.
The next lemma, also using the Helffer–Sjöstrand formula, will allow us to convert this intermediary local law into a moment estimate for
L_{N}(f)
in the specific case where
f
is a truncated version of
f_{\alpha,w}
defined in (
2.4
).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
p>2
.
For any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, any
\delta>0
and any
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
supported in
[-1-\delta,1+\delta]
, there exists
C>0
such that the following holds.
For any
N,q\geq 1
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, if there exists
M>0
such that, for any
x\in[-1-\delta,1+\delta]
and
y\in(0,\delta]
, with
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\frac{M^{q}}{N^{q}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{y}+\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}}}\right)^{q},

(3.10)

then, for any
w\in K
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,w}\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq C^{q}M^{q}.

(3.11)

We make use in the proof of Lemma
4.2
, which is stated in the next section and contains bounds on
f_{\alpha,w}
and its derivatives.

Proof.

Let
\chi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathbbm{1}_{[-\delta/2,\delta/2]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-\delta,\delta]}
.
First, by (
3.3
), denoting for
z=x+\mathrm{i}y

F_{\alpha,w}(z)\coloneqq\mathrm{i}y(f_{\alpha,w}\phi)^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)+\mathrm{i}(f_{\alpha,w}\phi)(x)+\mathrm{i}y(f_{\alpha,w}\phi)^{\prime}(x))\chi^{\prime}(y),

we get:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\pi}{N}}}\right)^{q}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,w}\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F_{\alpha,w}(z)(s_{N}-s_{V_{\alpha}})(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{q}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert F_{\alpha,w}(z_{i})}}\right\rvert}}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{i=1}^{q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z_{i})-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{i})}}\right\rvert}}\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{q}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{N}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert F_{\alpha,w}(z)}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert}+\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}}\right)^{q},

(3.12)

where in the last line we used Hölder’s inequality as in (
3.7
) and the assumption (
3.10
).
It remains to bound the integral on the right-hand side.
Bounding
\chi(y)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\delta}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\chi^{\prime}(y)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\in[\delta/2,\delta]}
, recalling that
\phi
is supported in
[-1-\delta,1+\delta]
with bounded derivatives and using Lemma
4.2
(which gives controls on
f_{\alpha,w}
and its derivatives), we get

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert F_{\alpha,w}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\delta}+\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\in[\delta/2,\delta]}}}\right).

Therefore, noting that
1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert+1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}\leq C
for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\delta/2
, we get

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert F_{\alpha,w}(z)}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert}+\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

(3.13)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\delta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\in[\delta/2,\delta]}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\log\frac{1}{x}}}\right)+1}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x,

(3.14)

using that
\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\delta}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert}{x^{2}+y^{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y=\log\frac{x^{2}+\delta^{2}}{x^{2}}
.
The integral on the RHS of (
3.14
) is a finite constant because
p>2
, so, coming back to (
3.12
), we get the result.
∎

4
Bounds on the inverse of the master operator

As explained in Section
1.4
, the control of the linear statistics
L_{N}(f)
via the master operator strategy requires to bound moments of
A_{N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]}}\right)
, where we recall
\Xi_{\alpha}
is the
master operator
defined in (
1.27
) as

\Xi_{\alpha}[\psi](x)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{2}\psi(x)V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)+\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\psi(x)-\psi(t)}{x-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t).

In fact, this operator is invertible
up to a constant
, meaning that for
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, there is a constant
a
(depending on
f
) such that the equation
\Xi_{\alpha}[\psi]=f-a
has a unique solution
\psi
, see Lemma
4.1
. The presence of this constant does not matter since if two functions
f
and
g
differ by a constant, then the linear statistics coincide:
L_{N}(f)=L_{N}(g)
.

This strategy will be used in Section
5
to improve the bounds on
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]
and in Section
6
to prove the CLT for a generic function
f\in\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
.
Therefore, in this section, we state bounds on the derivatives of
\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]
both for generic
f\in\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
in Lemma
4.1
and for
f=f_{\alpha,z}
in Lemma
4.3
(note that
f_{\alpha,z}\notin\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
, see Lemma
4.2
).
In particular, as mentioned in Remark
3.5
, in order to control
A_{N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]}}\right)

via
the use of Helffer–Sjöstrand formula and local laws, we need to prove that
\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]^{\prime\prime}
is locally integrable, which is indeed the case.
Finally, as explained in Remarks
3.3
and
3.5
, a regularization argument is needed before applying Helffer–Sjöstrand formula. The tool for this argument is proved in Lemma
4.5
.
Most proofs are postponed to Appendix
A
.

4.1
Inverse of the master operator

We state here the well-known inversion of the master operator
\Xi_{\alpha}
and prove its continuity with respect to
\mathcal{C}^{k}
-norms defined by
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k}}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{k}\lVert f^{(i)}\rVert_{\infty}
. This type of results dates back to
[
56
, Eq. (12), p.175]
and
[
8
, Lemma 3.2]
, but we rely here on arguments of
[
9
, Lemma 3.3]
.
The proof is postponed to Appendix
A.3
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
p>2
.
There exists
C>0
depending only on
p
such that the following holds.

(i)

Let
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
. Then, letting
a\coloneqq\int_{-1}^{1}f(t)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}
, the function
\psi_{\alpha}
defined by

\psi_{\alpha}(\lambda)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\pi r_{\alpha}(\lambda)}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{f(t)-f(\lambda)}{t-\lambda}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},&\text{if }\lambda\in[-1,1],\\
\displaystyle\frac{\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\psi_{\alpha}(t)}{\lambda-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)+f(\lambda)-a}{\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\lambda-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)-\frac{1}{2}V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda)},&\text{if }\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1],\end{cases}

(4.1)

is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
on
\mathbb{R}
, satisfies
\Xi_{\alpha}[\psi_{\alpha}]=f-a
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\psi_{\alpha}}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{2}}
. We write
\psi_{\alpha}=\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]
, with a slight abuse of notation.

(ii)

If moreover
f\in\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
, then
\psi_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
and, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\psi_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{3}}\times\begin{cases}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3})&\text{if }p<3,\\
(1+(\log 1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)_{+})&\text{if }p=3,\\
1&\text{if }p>3.\end{cases}

4.2
Bounds on
f_{\alpha,z}
and its preimage via the master operator

The following lemma establishes bounds on
f_{\alpha,z}
, defined in (
2.4
), and its derivatives on compact sets.
It is proved in Appendix
A.4
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
p>2
.
For all
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
f_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
.
Moreover, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in K\cap\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)^{-(3-p)_{+}},

and, if
\lambda\neq 0
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}.

As mentioned at the beginning of the section, in order to control the linear statistics
L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})
appearing in the loop equations, we need to study the function

\psi_{\alpha,z}\coloneqq\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f_{\alpha,z}],

(4.2)

which is well-defined by Lemma
4.1
because
f_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
The next lemma establishes bounds on
\psi_{\alpha,z}
and its first two derivatives.
It is also proved in Appendix
A.4
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
p>2
.
For all
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\psi_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
.
Moreover, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in K\cap\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\psi_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\psi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,

and, if
\lambda\neq 0
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\psi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\times\begin{cases}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3})&\text{if }p<3,\\
(1+(\log 1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)_{+})&\text{if }p=3,\\
1&\text{if }p>3.\end{cases}.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}
such that

\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]}\leq\phi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0},1+\delta_{0}]},

where
\delta_{0}
is as in Lemma
2.6
. From Lemma
4.3
, we deduce the following regularity properties for

\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}\coloneqq\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f_{\alpha,z}\phi].

(4.3)

In contrast with the estimates of Lemma
4.3
, the regularity bounds for
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)
are valid for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
p>2
.
For all
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
.
Moreover, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert},\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,

and, if
\lambda\neq 0
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\times\begin{cases}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3})&\text{if }p<3,\\
(1+(\log 1/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)_{+})&\text{if }p=3,\\
1&\text{if }p>3.\end{cases}.

Proof.

In view of (
4.1
), and since
\phi=1
on
[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]
, we have
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}=\psi_{\alpha,z}
on
[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]
. The claimed bounds for
\lambda\in(-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2)
follow from Lemma
4.3
. We now check that these bounds hold for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]
.
The denominator in the definition of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)
in (
4.1
) satisfies, by (
A.13
) and then Lemma
A.3
, for any
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\delta_{0}/2
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\lambda-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)-\frac{1}{2}V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert=r_{\alpha}(\lambda)\sqrt{\lambda^{2}-1}\geq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\alpha\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-2}}}\right)\geq c(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert).

(4.4)

Now, denote

\mathcal{I}_{\alpha,z}\colon\lambda\mapsto\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(t)}{\lambda-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t),\qquad\mathcal{J}_{\alpha,z}\colon\lambda\mapsto\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\lambda-t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t).

By differentiation under the integral and a crude bound, one checks that both functions are twice differentiable (even
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
) on
\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1-\delta_{0}/4,1+\delta_{0}/4]
, with
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{(n)}(\lambda)\rvert,\lvert\mathcal{J}^{(n)}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C
for
n\in\{0,1,2\}
with some
C
independent of
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
(in fact, one checks by dominated convergence that
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{(n)}(\lambda)\rvert,\lvert\mathcal{J}^{(n)}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{n}
).
Recalling the definition of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
in (
4.1
), the result follows by direct computation of the derivatives of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
, using (
4.4
) to lower bound the denominator.
∎

4.3
Regularizing test functions

As explained in Remark
3.5
, we need to regularize test functions before applying Helffer–Sjöstrand formula
via
Lemma
3.4
.
This regularization argument is contained in the result below, the function
\varphi
has to be thought as
\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f]
for either a generic
f\in\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
or
f=f_{\alpha,z}
.

Lemma 4.5

(Regularization lemma)
.

Let
\gamma\in[0,1)
.
Let
M>0
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq M(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma})
for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, there exists
\varphi_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that

\supp\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset\supp\varphi+[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon],\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi-\varphi_{\varepsilon}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty},\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq CM\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}

and, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\varepsilon)^{-\gamma}}}\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{CM}{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}}}\right),

where
C>0
depends only on
\gamma
.

Proof.

Let
\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\supp\xi\subset[-1,1]
,
0\leq\xi\leq 1
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\xi(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x=1
.
For
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, let
\xi_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\xi(\cdot/\varepsilon)
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}=\varphi*\xi_{\varepsilon}
.
Then, these are standard facts that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
\supp\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset\supp\varphi+[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi-\varphi_{\varepsilon}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}=\varphi^{\prime}*\xi_{\varepsilon}
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}=\varphi^{\prime\prime}*\xi_{\varepsilon}
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\varphi^{\prime\prime}*\xi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. We have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime}(\lambda)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\prime}(\lambda)-\varphi^{\prime}(\lambda-x))\xi_{\varepsilon}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x}}\right\rvert\leq\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\lambda-\varepsilon}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}}\right)\xi_{\varepsilon}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x,

and, by assumption,

\int_{\lambda-\varepsilon}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq\int_{\lambda-\varepsilon}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}M(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq 2\varepsilon M+\begin{cases}2\varepsilon M\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda/2}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}&\text{if }\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq 2\varepsilon,\\
\frac{2}{1-\gamma}M(3\varepsilon)^{1-\gamma}&\text{if }\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert<2\varepsilon,\end{cases}

(4.5)

bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert-\varepsilon\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda/2}}\right\rvert
in the first case and computing explicitly the integral
\int_{-3\varepsilon}^{3\varepsilon}M\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u
in the second one.
In particular, this proves
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq CM\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}
because
\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\xi_{\varepsilon}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x=1
.
For the 2nd derivative, using
\xi\leq 1
, we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\lambda-x)\xi_{\varepsilon}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\lambda-\varepsilon}^{\lambda+\varepsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\varepsilon)^{-\gamma}}}\right),

where the last inequality follows from (
4.5
).
The bound on the third derivative follows similarly, by using
\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\varphi^{\prime\prime}*\xi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
together with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\xi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\xi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}/\varepsilon^{2}
.
∎

5
The bootstrap argument to get the local law

We now have the main ingredients to run the local law machinery. We will state the first
a priori
bounds on the moments of
L_{N}(f)
and
A_{N}(f)
, defined in (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), and a truncation Lemma that will allow us to only consider compactly supported functions. Together these results will allow us to deduce a first bound
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]
and thus a first local law via Proposition
2.7
. We will see how to deduce a better
a priori
bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]
in Lemma
5.6
via the use of Helffer–Sjöstrand formula and the bounds proven in the previous section.

5.1
A priori bounds

We first gather known bounds to control outliers.
The first one bounds the tail of the one-point function
p_{N}^{\alpha}
, which is the density of the marginal distribution of
\lambda_{1}
under
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
.
The second one bounds the probability of having an outlier, hence providing a control closer to the limiting spectrum
[-1,1]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
p>1
.

(i)

There exist
X_{1}\geq 1
and
c_{1}>0

such that, for any
N\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\geq X_{1}
,

p_{N}^{\alpha}(x)\leq e^{-c_{1}NV_{\alpha}(x)}.

(ii)

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
C,c_{2}>0
such that, for any
N\geq 1
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}(\exists k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\varepsilon)\leq Ce^{-c_{2}N}.

Proof.

Part
(i)
. This is proved in
[
50
, Theorem 2.2.(i)]
: the fact that
X_{1}
can be chosen uniformly for
\alpha\in[0,1]
is explicitly stated there, and taking a look at their proof shows that this is also the case for
c_{1}
, which ultimately depends on the constants they call
\beta
,
\varepsilon
,
m
,
M
.

Part
(ii)
. This follows from the large deviation principle for the extreme eigenvalues proved for example in
[
16
, Proposition 2.1]
. Uniformity in
\alpha
of
C
and
c_{2}
is not stated there, but it follows from the proof that the large deviation principle holds uniformly in
\alpha
: we deduce that, uniformly in
\alpha
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}(\exists k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\varepsilon)\leq-\inf_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\varepsilon}V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(x)=-\inf_{x\geq 1+\varepsilon}V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(x),

where
V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}
was defined in (
1.19
) and we used that it is even. Now by strict convexity of
V_{\alpha}
,
V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}
is strictly convex on
[1,\infty)
, but it is also non-negative and equal to 0 at 1, so it is increasing on
[1,\infty)
.
Therefore,
\inf_{x\geq 1+\varepsilon}V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(x)=V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(1+\varepsilon)>0
.
Moreover,
\alpha\in[0,1]\mapsto V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(1+\varepsilon)
is continuous so the following minimum is well-defined and positive:

c_{2}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\min_{\alpha\in[0,1]}V_{{\mathrm{eff}},\alpha}(1+\varepsilon)>0,

and we deduce the result with this choice of
c_{2}
.
∎

We now state first rough
a priori
bounds on moments of linear statistics and anisotropy terms.
They are obtained as a consequence of
[
31
, Corollary 4.16]
, which is proved for any
\mathcal{C}^{1}
potential
V
.

Lemma 5.2

(
A priori
bounds)
.

Let
p>1
. Let
K\subset\mathbb{R}
be a compact set.
There exists
C>0
such that, for any
N\geq 2
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
q\geq 1
, the following holds.

(i)

For any
f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp f\subset K
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(C\sqrt{qN\log N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}}}\right)^{q}.

(ii)

For any
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp f\subset K
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(CqN\log N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{2}}}}\right)^{q}.

Proof.

This result is a consequence of
[
31
, Corollary 4.16]
, which is stated for a single
\mathcal{C}^{1}
potential
V
, but actually holds for the potentials
V_{\alpha}
uniformly in
\alpha
.
Indeed, a read through the proofs of Lemma 4.14 and of its consequence Corollary 4.16 in
[
31
]
shows that the constants appearing in these results can be chosen uniformly for any class of potentials
\mathcal{V}
such that there exists
M,C,c>0
such that, for any
V\in\mathcal{V}
,
\mathbb{P}(\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{i}}}\right\rvert>M)\leq Ce^{-cN}
and
\sup_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq M+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\leq C
.

Part
(i)
.
In the first part of
[
31
, Corollary 4.16]
, two norms appear: The Lipschitz norm
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{L}
which is by Taylor’s inequality less than
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}
, and the Sobolev
1/2
-norm
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{1/2}
which is by
[
31
, Property 4.13]
less than
2(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}})\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}
with
C
depending on the length of
K
.
Hence, this result implies that there exists
C_{0},c_{0}>0
independent of
\alpha
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert\geq C_{0}\sqrt{N\log N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}}}\right)\leq e^{-c_{0}N}.

Bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert\leq N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
on this event, it follows that

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(C_{0}\sqrt{N\log N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}}}\right)^{q}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}}\right)^{q}e^{-c_{0}N}.

(5.1)

On the other hand, note that
(c_{0}N)^{q}/q!\leq e^{c_{0}N}
, so we have
N^{q}\leq(q/c_{0})^{q}e^{c_{0}N}
, which yields
N^{q/2}\leq(q/c_{0})^{q/2}e^{c_{0}N/2}
.
Applying this to the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.1
) yields the result.

Part
(ii)
. The proof is similar: the second part of
[
31
, Corollary 4.16]
shows that there exists
C_{0},c_{0}>0
independent of
\alpha
such that
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert\geq C_{0}N\log N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{2}}}}\right)\leq e^{-c_{0}N}
.
We combine this with the deterministic bound
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert\leq 4N^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
and then the bound
N^{q}\leq(q/c_{0})^{q}e^{c_{0}N}
as before.
∎

In order to restrict ourselves to compactly supported test functions, we also need the following result, which is obtained as a consequence of results in
[
24
]
.

Lemma 5.3

(Truncation lemma)
.

Let
p>1
.
Let
\delta_{0}>0
be the constant given by Lemma
2.6
.
There exist
C,c>0
such that the following holds for any

N,q\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]}\leq\phi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0},1+\delta_{0}]}.

(5.2)

(i)

For any
M>0
and any
f\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f(x)}}\right\rvert\leq M(1+V_{\alpha}(x))
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CMq)^{q}e^{-cN}.

(ii)

If
q\leq\sqrt{N}
, then for any
M,\gamma>0
and any
f\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f(x)}}\right\rvert\leq Me^{\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CM)^{q}e^{-cN}.

(iii)

For any
M>0
and any differentiable
f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\leq M
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f(x)}}\right\rvert\leq M
for all
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta_{0}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)-A_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}}\right))^{q}q^{2q}e^{-cN}.

Proof.

Part
(i)
.
The proof proceeds in two steps: we first restrict the support to a large but bounded interval, and then to
[-1-\delta_{0},1+\delta_{0}]
.
For the first step, let
X_{1}\geq 1
be the constant appearing in Lemma
5.1
.
(i)
and
\phi_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

\mathbbm{1}_{[-X_{1},X_{1}]}\leq\phi_{0}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-X_{1}-1,X_{1}+1]}.

Then, using the assumption on
f
, we get

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}M(V_{\alpha}(\lambda_{k})+1)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq X_{1}}}}\right)^{q}\leq N^{q-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}M^{q}(V_{\alpha}(\lambda_{k})+1)^{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq X_{1}},

by Jensen’s inequality.
Using Lemma
5.1
.
(i)
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(NM)^{q}\int_{X_{1}}^{\infty}(V_{\alpha}(x)+1)^{q}e^{-c_{1}NV_{\alpha}(x)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2(NM)^{q}\int_{NV_{\alpha}(X_{1})}^{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Cy}{N}}}\right)^{q}e^{-c_{1}y}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{NV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(X_{1})},

using that
V_{\alpha}(x)+1\leq CV_{\alpha}(x)
for
x\geq X_{1}\geq 1
and changing variables with
y=NV_{\alpha}(x)
, noting that
V_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is increasing on
[X_{1},\infty)
to bound
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\leq\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y/[NV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(X_{1})]
.
Using
y^{q}\leq(2q/c_{1})^{q}e^{c_{1}y/2}
and
NV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(X_{1})\geq c
, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CMq)^{q}\int_{NV_{\alpha}(X_{1})}^{\infty}e^{-c_{1}y/2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\leq(CMq)^{q}e^{-c_{1}NV_{\alpha}(X_{1})/2},

(5.3)

which concludes the first step of the proof.
Now, consider
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
as in the statement.
By Lemma
5.1
.
(ii)
, there exists
C,c_{2}>0
independent of
\alpha
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}(\exists k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq 1+\delta_{0}/2)\leq Ce^{-c_{2}N}.

(5.4)

Bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f\phi_{0})-L_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert\leq NM((X_{1}+1)^{2}+1)
on the event above, and noting that
L_{N}(f\phi_{0})=L_{N}(f\phi)
on the complement, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f\phi_{0})-L_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CNM)^{q}e^{-c_{2}N}\leq(CMq)^{q}e^{-c_{2}N/2},

(5.5)

using that
N^{q}\leq(2q/c_{2})^{q}e^{c_{2}N/2}
.
Combining (
5.3
) and (
5.5
) yields Part
(i)
.

Part
(ii)
. We follow the same two steps as before.
In the first step, we bound

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}Me^{\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq X_{1}}}}\right)^{q}\leq N^{q-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}M^{q}e^{\gamma q\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert\geq X_{1}},

by Jensen’s inequality again. Then, using Lemma
5.1
.
(i)
, we deduce

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi_{0})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq 2(NM)^{q}\int_{X_{1}}^{\infty}e^{\gamma qx-c_{1}Nx^{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\leq(CMN)^{q}e^{-c_{1}\gamma NX_{1}^{2}/2},

(5.6)

where in the second inequality we noted that, by choosing
X_{1}\geq 2\gamma/c_{1}
, we have
e^{\gamma qx-c_{1}Nx^{2}}\leq e^{-\gamma c_{1}NX_{1}x/2}
for any
x\geq X_{1}
and
q\leq\sqrt{N}\leq N
. This concludes the first step.
For the second step, we use the first inequality in (
5.5
) and conclude that, for some
C,c>0
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f)-L_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CMN)^{q}e^{-cN}\leq(CM)^{q}e^{-cN},

up to a modifications of
C,c
in the second inequality where we used
q\leq\sqrt{N}
.

Part
(iii)
.
Note that on the event
\{\forall k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda_{k}}}\right\rvert<1+\delta_{0}/2\}
, we have
A_{N}(f)=A_{N}(f\phi)
. On the complement, we bound deterministically

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)-A_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert\leq 4N^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert(f\phi)^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}}\right),

and then apply Lemma
5.1
.
(ii)
.
Moreover, we note that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert(f\phi)^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f^{\prime}\phi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f\phi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq M(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty})
using the assumption on
f
and that
\phi^{\prime}=0
outside
[-1-\delta_{0},1+\delta_{0}]
.
This yields

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(f)-A_{N}(f\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(CM\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}}\right))^{q}N^{2q}e^{-c_{2}N}.

Using that
N^{2q}\leq(4q/c_{2})^{2q}e^{c_{2}N/2}
concludes the proof.
∎

The two previous results allow us to deduce a first
a priori
bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]
. This bound is very crude and will be improved later.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
p>2
.
Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a compact set. Then there exists
C>0
such that, for any
N\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
q\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(Cq\sqrt{N\log N})^{q}.

Proof.

Let
M\geq 1
be such that
\re K\subset[-M,M]
.
By Lemma
4.2
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in[-2M,2M]
,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C
.
Moreover, for
\lambda\notin[-2M,2M]
, we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert=\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g_{\alpha}(\lambda)-g_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda-z}}\right\rvert}\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g_{\alpha}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert/2}\leq\frac{CV_{\alpha}(\lambda)}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert},

using
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g_{\alpha}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq CV_{\alpha}(\lambda)
and that
g_{\alpha}
is bounded on
K
(see Remark
2.2
).
In particular, this proves there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C(V_{\alpha}(\lambda)+1)
.
Therefore, by Lemma
5.3
.
(i)
, for any
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying (
5.2
), there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
N,q\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq 2^{q}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z}\phi)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]+(Cq)^{q}.

(5.7)

Then, the result follows from Lemma
5.2
.
(i)
, noting that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f_{\alpha,z}\phi}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}
is uniformly bounded for
z\in K
by Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Combining this with Proposition
2.7
.
(i)
shows the following result.
Note that we do not apply Proposition
2.7
.
(ii)
here to obtain a local law outside of the trapezoid region
\mathcal{T}
, because the resulting bound would be too bad due to the fact that
B_{\max}=C\sqrt{N\log N}
appears squared in this bound.
In the proof of Lemma
5.6
below, we use instead the
a priori
bounds of Lemma
5.2
to control what happens outside of
[-1,1]
.

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
p>2
.
Let
\delta_{0}
and
\mathcal{T}
be as in Lemma
2.6
.
There exists
C>0
such that, for any
N\geq 2
,
q\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in\mathcal{T}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert s_{N}(z)-s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Cq\sqrt{\log N}}{\sqrt{N}y}}}\right)^{q}.

5.2
Master operator approach to bound
L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})

We now explain in this subsection how to improve the bound on
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})^{q}}}\right]
using Corollary
5.5
.
For this, we use the master operator strategy, using a bound on the Laplace transform of
L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})
obtained by a change of variables in the partition function
Z_{N}^{\alpha}
.
This strategy is used for example in
[
9
, Proposition 4.1]
or
[
31
, Lemma 4.24]
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
p>2
.
Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}
be a compact set. Then there exists
C>0
such that, for any
N\geq 2
,
q\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in K
,

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})|^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Cq^{2}(\log N)^{2}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}}}\right)^{q}.

(5.8)

Proof.

Let
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0}/2,1+\delta_{0}/2]}\leq\phi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-1-\delta_{0},1+\delta_{0}]}
, where
\delta_{0}
is as in Lemma
2.6
, and recall
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}\coloneqq\Xi_{\alpha}^{-1}[f_{\alpha,z}\phi]
. All constants
C
appearing below are independent of
\alpha
and
z
. We make the change of variables

\lambda_{k}=y_{k}+\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})}{N},\quad 1\leq k\leq N,

in the partition function

Z_{N}^{\alpha}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}|\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{\ell}|^{\beta}}}\right)e^{-\frac{\beta N}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}(\lambda_{k})}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}\ldots\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{N}.

(5.9)

By Corollary
4.4
, there is some
C>0
such that for
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)|\leq C
, so this indeed defines a diffeomorphic change of variables for
N
large enough (for
N
small, the result is a consequence of Corollary
5.4
). We find

Z_{N}^{\alpha}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|y_{k}-y_{\ell}+\frac{1}{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{\ell}))}}\right|}}\right)^{\beta}e^{-\frac{\beta N}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}(y_{k}+\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})/N)}\times\\
\times\prod_{k=1}^{N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(y_{k})/N}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y_{k}.

(5.10)

Factoring out
\prod_{k<\ell}|y_{k}-y_{\ell}|^{\beta}
in the product term, and Taylor expanding the different terms involved, we will now convert this rewriting into an expectation with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}
. By Taylor’s theorem, there exist
\theta_{k,\ell}\in[0,1]
such that

\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|1+\frac{1}{N}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{\ell})}{y_{k}-y_{\ell}}}}\right|=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{\ell})}{y_{k}-y_{\ell}}\\
-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\theta_{k,\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{\ell})}{y_{k}-y_{\ell}}}}\right)^{2}

and
\theta_{k}\in[0,1]
such that

V_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{k}+\frac{1}{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})}}\right)=V_{\alpha}(y_{k})+\frac{1}{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(y_{k})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})^{2}V_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{k}+\frac{\theta_{k}}{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(y_{k})}}\right).

Using this and dividing both sides of (
5.10
) by
Z_{N}^{\alpha}
, we obtain:

1=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\beta}{N}\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{\ell})}{\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{\ell}}-\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{k})V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda_{k})}}\right)\times e^{\mathrm{Rem}_{N}}}}\right],

(5.11)

where the remainder term
\mathrm{Rem}_{N}
can be bounded as follows

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathrm{Rem}_{N}}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{|h|\leq\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}\|_{\infty}/N}\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{2}V_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot+h)\|_{\infty}+\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}}}\right)+\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq C,

(5.12)

for some finite constant
C
by Corollary
4.4
, which in particular implies that, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert h}}\right\rvert\leq 1
(we use here
\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}\|_{\infty}/N\leq 1
for
N
large enough),

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(x)^{2}V_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(x+h)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(x+h)}}\right\rvert}{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert)^{2}}\leq\frac{C(\alpha\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x+h}}\right\rvert^{p-2}+(1-\alpha))}{(1+\alpha\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-1}+(1-\alpha)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert)^{2}}\leq C.

Now, recalling the definition of
\Xi_{\alpha}
in (
1.27
) and of the anisotropy in (
3.2
), and using (
1.23
), notice that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq N}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{k})-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{\ell})}{\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{\ell}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}(\lambda_{k})V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\lambda_{k})\\
=L_{N}(\Xi_{\alpha}[\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}])+\frac{1}{2N}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda_{k})}}\right).

Moreover, recall that
\Xi_{\alpha}[\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}]=f_{\alpha,z}\phi
. Therefore, coming back to (
5.11
), using (
5.12
) and bounding
\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda_{k})\rvert\leq\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq C
,
we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z}\phi)+\frac{1}{2N}A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right)}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L_{N}(\Xi_{\alpha}[\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}])+\frac{1}{2N}A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right)}}\right]\leq C.

Replacing
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
by
-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
in the change of variables, we get the same bound with a minus sign in the exponential. therefore, using that
e^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert}\leq e^{x}+e^{-x}
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we find that

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z}\phi)+\frac{1}{2N}A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert}}\right)}}\right]\leq C.

Expanding the exponential in series, we deduce that for all
q\geq 1

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{1}{q!}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z}\phi)-\frac{1}{2N}A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right|^{q}}}\right]\leq C.

Combining this with (
5.7
), we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|L_{N}(f_{\alpha,z})}}\right|^{q}}}\right]\leq(Cq)^{q}+\frac{C^{q}}{N^{q}}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right].

(5.13)

It remains to bound the anisotropy term. We use two successive truncations. Firstly, applying Lemma
5.3
.
(iii)
(recall
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
and its derivative are bounded by Corollary
4.4
), we get

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})-A_{N}(\phi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq(Cq)^{2q}.

(5.14)

Then, we would like to use the local law in Corollary
5.5
together with Lemma
3.4
, but this local law is true only in the trapezoid region so we have to reduce again the support so that it is contained in
[-1,1]
(with some margin for the regularization step).
So we consider
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
such that

\mathbbm{1}_{[-1/4,1/4]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-1/2,1/2]}.

Noting that
\phi-\chi=0
on
[-1/4,1/4]
, we get, by Corollary
4.4
, that
(\phi-\chi)\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
is in
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with bounded derivatives (uniformly in
\alpha
and
z\in K
), therefore we can apply Lemma
5.2
.
(ii)
to bound

\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}(\phi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})-A_{N}(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert A_{N}((\phi-\chi)\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})}}\right\rvert^{q}}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(CqN\log N}}\right)^{q}.

(5.15)

Now, we want to apply Lemma
3.4
to
\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
, but it requires
\mathcal{C}^{3}
regularity, so we first use a regularization argument.
By Corollary
4.4
again, with
\gamma=(3-p)_{+}
if
p\neq 3
and some arbitrary
\gamma\in(0,1)
if
p=3
, we have, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lvert(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})(\lambda)\rvert\leq C,\qquad\lvert(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})^{\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C,\qquad\lvert(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}).

(5.16)

Let
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be the regularization of
\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}
given by Lemma
4.5
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
, then
\supp\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon}\subset\supp\chi+[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]\subset[-1,1]
,

\lVert\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon\lVert(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon,\qquad\lVert(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})^{\prime}-(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\varepsilon^{1-\gamma},

(5.17)

and, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

|(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)|\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}),\qquad|(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-\gamma}).

(5.18)

Then,

|A_{N}(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z})|\leq|A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})|+|A_{N}(\chi\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}-\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})|\leq|A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})|+CN^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert},

(5.19)

where we used that
|A_{N}(f)|\leq 4N^{2}\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
. Now, with the local law in Corollary
5.5
as input and noting that
\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon}
is supported in
[-1,1]
, we can apply Lemma
3.4
with
K_{0}=[-1,1]
and
M=Cq\sqrt{N\log N}
to deduce that for any
\eta\in(0,(\delta_{0}\wedge 1)/2)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[|A_{N}(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})|^{q}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Cq^{2}N\log N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon}\|_{1}+\|(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}\|_{1}+\log(1/\eta)\|(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}+\eta\|(\widetilde{\psi}_{\alpha,z}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{1}}}\right)}}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Cq^{2}N\log N\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}+\frac{\log(1/\eta)}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}}}\right)}}\right)^{q},

(5.20)

where we used (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) to bound the
L^{1}
norms.
We now choose
\varepsilon,\eta
so that
N^{2}\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}\leq N\log N
and
\eta/\varepsilon\leq 1
. Taking for example
\varepsilon=\eta=((\log N)/N)^{1/(1-\gamma)}
and combining (
5.19
) and (
5.20
), we get
