\displaystyle\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{2}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3},

(A.17)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g^{\prime\prime}(t)-g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}.

(A.18)

Proof.

Recall our notation for Taylor expansions in (
A.1
).
For
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, by Taylor integral formula, we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g^{(k)}(t)-T_{2-k}[g^{(k)},\lambda](t)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{2-k}}{(2-k)!}\int_{\lambda\wedge t}^{\lambda\vee t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)}}\right\rvert\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u.

Hence, it is enough to prove that there exists
C>0
such that, for any
t,\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\int_{\lambda\wedge t}^{\lambda\vee t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}.

(A.19)

If
p\geq 3
, this obtained directly by bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\leq(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}
, so we now focus on the case
p\in(2,3)
.
By symmetries, we can assume that
\lambda<t
and that
t>0
. We distinguish cases:

•

If
\lambda<0
, then

\int_{\lambda}^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u=\frac{1}{p-2}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-2}+t^{p-2})\leq C(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-2}\leq C(t-\lambda)(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}
,
because
t-\lambda\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert
in that case.

•

If
\lambda>0
and
t\in(\lambda,2\lambda]
, then
\int_{\lambda}^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq(t-\lambda)\lambda^{p-3}\leq C(t-\lambda)(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}
,
using
\lambda^{p-3}\leq Ct^{p-3}
because
t\leq 2\lambda
.

•

If
\lambda>0
and
t>2\lambda
, then

\int_{\lambda}^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u\leq Ct^{p-2}\leq C(t-\lambda)t^{p-3}=C(t-\lambda)(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}
,
where in the second inequality we used that
t-\lambda\geq t/2
in that case.

Therefore, we proved (
A.19
) and the result follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Recall the definition of
f_{\alpha,z}
in (
2.4
).
Note that
f_{\alpha,z}=\alpha f_{1,z}+(1-\alpha)f_{0,z}
, so it is enough to deal with the case
\alpha=0
and
\alpha=1
.
But the case
\alpha=0
is immediate, because
f_{0,z}(\lambda)=4(\lambda+z)
, so we now focus on the case
\alpha=1
.
The regularity of
f_{1,z}
directly follows from the one of
g
.
Notice that for
k\in\{0,1,2\}
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

f_{1,z}^{(k)}(\lambda)=(-1)^{k}\frac{k!}{(z-\lambda)^{k+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(z)-T_{k}[g,\lambda](z)}}\right),

where the notation
T_{k}[g,\lambda](z)
is introduced in (
A.1
). The same formula holds for
k=3
and
\lambda\neq 0
.
We now consider
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in K\cap\mathbb{R}
and bound
\lvert f_{1,z}^{(k)}(\lambda)\rvert
successively for
k\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.

Case
k=0
.

Injecting the definition of
g(z)
for complex
z
in (
2.2
),

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g(x)-g(\lambda)+\mathrm{i}yg^{\prime}(x)-y^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)/2-\mathrm{i}y^{3}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y/x)/6}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x-\lambda}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert g^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{[-M,M],\infty}+\frac{y^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert g^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{[-M,M],\infty}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert^{p\vee 3}}}\right),

with
M
such that
\re K\subset[-M,M]
, where we used Taylor–Lagrange inequality for the term involving
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g(x)-g(\lambda)}}\right\rvert
and (
2.7
) for the one involving
g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)
. This proves
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C
because
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x-\lambda}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert
.

Case
k=1
.
Similarly, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(x)-g(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right)+\mathrm{i}y(g^{\prime}(x)-g^{\prime}(\lambda))-\frac{y^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)-\mathrm{i}\frac{y^{3}}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\chi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{y}{x}}}\right)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{(x-\lambda)^{2}}{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert|x-\lambda|+\frac{y^{2}}{2}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert g^{\prime\prime}}}\right\rVert_{[-M,M],\infty}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert^{p\vee 3}}}\right),

which yields
\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C
as before.

Case
k=2
.
Here, we decompose

\displaystyle f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(z-\lambda)^{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(z)-g(\lambda)-(z-\lambda)g^{\prime}(\lambda)-\frac{(z-\lambda)^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right)=\frac{2}{(z-\lambda)^{3}}(\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III}+\mathrm{IV}),

with

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\coloneqq g(x)-g(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)/2,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{i}y\big(g^{\prime}(x)-g^{\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-y^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{\prime\prime}(x)-g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right)/2,

\displaystyle\mathrm{IV}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-\mathrm{i}y^{3}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y/x)/6.

By Lemma
A.5
, the first three terms are bounded in absolute value by

C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{3}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{3}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)^{-(3-p)_{+}},

(A.20)

bounding
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)^{p-3}\leq C
with
C
depending on
K
when
p\geq 3
.
It remains to bound
\mathrm{IV}
.
When
p\geq 3
, by (
2.7
), we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathrm{IV}}}\right\rvert\leq Cy^{3}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{3}
.
Now assume
p<3
. We distinguish two cases. If
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert
, then we bound
\chi\leq 1
and get
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathrm{IV}}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-3}
, which is itself bounded by (
A.20
) in that case.
On the other hand, if
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert>2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert
, then
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x-\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert/2
, so using again (
2.7
), we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathrm{IV}}}\right\rvert\leq Cy^{p}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3}
, which is also bounded by (
A.20
) in that case.
Combining these yields
\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert)^{-(3-p)_{+}}
as desired.

Case
k=3
.
We assume here that
\lambda\neq 0
. Using the same bound as for
k=2
for the first terms and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}
for the last term, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{6}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(z)-g(\lambda)-(z-\lambda)g^{\prime}(\lambda)-\frac{(z-\lambda)^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right)-\frac{(z-\lambda)^{3}}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert}.

If
\lvert x-\lambda\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert/2
, then
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert/2
, so we get
\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3}/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
.
If
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert
, then
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert/\sqrt{5}
and we also get the desired result.
It remains to cover the case where
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda-x}}\right\rvert<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert/2
and
2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert
. Here we decompose differently:

\displaystyle f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)=\frac{6}{(z-\lambda)^{4}}(\mathrm{I^{\prime}}+\mathrm{II^{\prime}}+\mathrm{III^{\prime}}+\mathrm{IV^{\prime}}),

with

\displaystyle\mathrm{I^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq g(x)-g(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)/2-(x-\lambda)^{3}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)/6,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{i}y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{\prime}(x)-g^{\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{III^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-y^{2}/2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{\prime\prime}(x)-g^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)-(x-\lambda)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{IV^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-\mathrm{i}y^{3}(g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda))/6,

where we used that
\chi(y/x)=1
when
2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert
.
The condition
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda-x}}\right\rvert<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert/2
yields that, on the interval between
\lambda
and
x
,
\lvert g^{(4)}\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-4}
and, using also that
2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert
implies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert/2
, we get
\lvert g^{(4)}\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3}/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
on this interval.
Therefore, applying Taylor–Lagrange inequality to the four terms, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert=\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((x-\lambda)^{4}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x-\lambda}}\right\rvert^{3}+y^{2}(x-\lambda)^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert y}}\right\rvert^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x-\lambda}}\right\rvert}}\right)\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{p-3}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\leq C\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\lambda}}\right\rvert^{(3-p)_{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert},

which concludes the proof.
∎

We now deal with the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
We first study the following function

\varphi_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)\coloneqq\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{f_{\alpha,z}(t)-f_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}{t-\lambda}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},\qquad\lambda\in\mathbb{R}.

(A.21)

Lemma A.6
.

Let
p>2
.
For any
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\varphi_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Moreover, for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
z\in K\setminus\mathbb{R}
and
\lambda\in[-1,1]
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.2
, we know that
f_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{\alpha,z}}}\right\rvert\leq C
and
\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}\rvert\leq C
on
[-1,1]
.
Hence, by Lemma
A.1
.
(i)
, we deduce that
\varphi_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}}}\right\rvert\leq C
on
[-1,1]
and, for
\lambda\in[-1,1]
,

\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{f_{\alpha,z}(t)-T_{1}[f_{\alpha,z},\lambda](t)}{(t-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{(t-\lambda)^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\lambda}^{t}(t-u)f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(u)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

using Taylor integral formula.
Bounding
\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(u)\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}
by Lemma
4.2
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-u}}\right\rvert\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert
, we get

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)}}\right\rvert\leq C\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\lambda}}\right\rvert}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\lambda\wedge t}^{\lambda\vee t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\leq C\int_{-1}^{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

(A.22)

by applying (
A.19
) if
p<3
and computing explicitly the integral w.r.t.
u
if
p\geq 3
. This proves

\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C
because
p>2
.

We now turn to the second derivative. Firstly, since
f_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
by Lemma
4.2
, we have
\varphi_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
by Lemma
A.2
.
(i)
and, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{f_{\alpha,z}(t)-T_{2}[f_{\alpha,z},\lambda](t)}{(t-\lambda)^{3}}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{(t-\lambda)^{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\lambda}^{t}\frac{(t-u)^{2}}{2}f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

by Taylor integral formula again.
Then, for
\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
, bounding
\lvert f_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert u}}\right\rvert^{-(3-p)_{+}}/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
by Lemma
4.2
and
(t-u)^{2}\leq(t-\lambda)^{2}
, and then proceeding as before in (
A.22
), we get
\lvert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\rvert\leq C/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
as desired.
The same inequalities provides the domination needed to prove convergence of
\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)
as
\lambda\to 0
from the dominated convergence theorem, proving
\varphi_{\alpha,z}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and concluding the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

The proof follows from the same lines as Lemma
4.1
.
(ii)
, even if here the function
f_{\alpha,z}
is not in
\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R})
, but only in
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{*})
. This regularity at 0 is only used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
when controlling
\lVert\varphi_{\alpha,z}^{\prime\prime}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{2}}
, but this has been done in Lemma
A.6
above.
The rest of the proof is identical replacing occurrences of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{3}}
by
C/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of results concerning the equilibrium Stieltjes transform

This section contains
the proof of Lemmas
2.5
and
2.6
, as well as some intermediary results.

B.1
Identities for the equilibrium Stieltjes transform

Proof of Lemma
2.5
.

Part
(i)
.

By differentiating (
1.19
), we have for
x\in(-1,1)

\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}{2}=\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)}{x-t},

(B.1)

and, for
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
, we find by integrating (
B.1
) against
\frac{1}{x-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}{x-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)=\int_{-1}^{1}\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)}{x-t}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)}{x-z}=-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{[-1,1]^{2}}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)}{(t-z)(x-z)}=-\dfrac{1}{2}s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)^{2},

where the second equality is obtained by symmetrizing the roles of
x
and
t
.
Therefore,

\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}{x-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)+s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)^{2}=0.

Now adding to this equation the equality

\frac{1}{z}\int_{-1}^{1}V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)=0,

obtained by integrating (
B.1
) against
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)
, we find that

0=\dfrac{1}{z}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{xV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}{x-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)+s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)^{2}=\dfrac{1}{z}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{xV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)-g_{\alpha}(z)}{x-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\alpha}}(x)+\dfrac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{z}s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)+s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)^{2}.

Finally, recalling that
g_{\alpha}(x)=xV^{\prime}_{\alpha}(x)
and the definition of
h_{\alpha}
in Definition
2.1
proves Part
(i)
.

Part
(ii)
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
. Using the definition of
r_{\alpha}
in (
1.25
) and that
\frac{1}{x-t}=\frac{t}{x(x-t)}+\frac{1}{x}
, we get

\displaystyle r_{\alpha}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)-V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{x-t}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)}{x-t}-\frac{tV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{x(x-t)}-\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{x}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi x}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{g_{\alpha}(x)-g_{\alpha}(t)}{x-t}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

where in the last equality we used that
xV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(x)=g_{\alpha}(x)
for real
x
, and that
V_{\alpha}^{\prime}/\sigma
is odd so that
\int_{-1}^{1}V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=0
. This shows that (
2.14
) is indeed an extension of the definition of
r_{\alpha}
in (
1.25
).

Part
(iii)
.

Since
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus[-1,1]
, we can use (
2.14
) and separate
r_{\alpha}
into

r_{\alpha}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{g_{\alpha}(t)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}-\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{2\pi z}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}.

(B.2)

Using
g_{\alpha}(t)=tV_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)
, we rewrite the first term as follows

\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{g_{\alpha}(t)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{t-z}+\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{z}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

using that
V_{\alpha}^{\prime}/\sigma
is an odd function.
The second term on the right-hand side of (
B.2
) is proportional to the Stieltjes transform of the arcsine law:

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{z-1}\sqrt{z+1}}.

(B.3)

Therefore, coming back to (
B.2
) and recalling the notation
b(z)=\sqrt{z-1}\sqrt{z+1}
, we obtain

r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)=\frac{b(z)}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}+\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{2z}.

Hence, it is now sufficient to show that

s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)=\frac{b(z)}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(t)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}.

(B.4)

Recalling (
1.24
), we have

\displaystyle s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)r_{\alpha}(t)}{t-z}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)}{t-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(u)}{u-t}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}{\sigma(u)}}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t,

where we used the definition of
r_{\alpha}(t)
in (
1.25
), together with the fact that
\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{t-u}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}{\sigma(u)}=0
for
t\in(-1,1)
.
Therefore, switching the order of the integrals (see
[
48
, Eq. (23.18)]
), we get

s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(u)}{\sigma(u)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)}{(t-z)(u-t)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u.

Moreover, by direct computation,

\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)}{(t-z)t(u-t)}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{u-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{t-z}+\fint_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sigma(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{u-t}}}\right)=\frac{1}{u-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b(z)-z}}\right)+\pi u}}\right)

and (
B.4
) follows using again that
\int_{-1}^{1}V_{\alpha}^{\prime}(u)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}{\sigma(u)}=0
.

Part
(iv)
.
This point comes from the general fact that if
\lambda=a-b/2
solves the quadratic equation
X^{2}+bX+c
, where
a,b,c\in\mathbb{C}
, then the other solution is given by
\mu=-a-b/2
.
∎

B.2
Proof of the stability lemma

The main goal of this section is to prove the stability lemma (Lemma
2.6
). The difference with the proof of the stability lemma in
[
19
]
is the lack of analyticity of
V_{\alpha}
and hence of
r_{\alpha}
.
We start with the following regularity bounds on
r_{\alpha}
, which would be obvious if
r_{\alpha}
analytic.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
p>2
.

(i)

For every compact
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z\in K
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im r_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im z}}\right\rvert.

(ii)

The function
r_{\alpha}
is continuous on
\mathbb{C}
uniformly in
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
i.e.
for all
\varepsilon>0
and
z\in\mathbb{C}
, there exists
\delta>0
such that for all
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
\forall z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}
,
|z-z^{\prime}|<\delta\Rightarrow|r_{\alpha}(z)-r_{\alpha}(z^{\prime})|<\varepsilon
.

(iii)

For any compact
K\subset\mathbb{C}
, there exist
c,\delta>0
such that for any
z\in K
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im{z}}}\right\rvert\leq\delta
, and for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
one has
\re r_{\alpha}(z)\geq c
.

Proof.

First note that
r_{\alpha}=\alpha r_{1}+2(1-\alpha)
, so it is enough to prove the result for
\alpha=1
. For brevity, we denote
r=r_{1}
.

Part
(i)
.

Write
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
. By symmetry, we can assume that
x,y\geq 0
. Moreover, if
y=0
, then
\im r(z)=0
so we can assume that
y>0
.
Recall the definition of
g(z)
in (
2.2
).
In order to treat the last term in
g(z)
separately, we define

\forall z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in\mathbb{C},\quad\widetilde{g}(z)\coloneqq g(x)+\mathrm{i}yg^{\prime}(x)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)=g(z)+\mathrm{i}\frac{y^{3}}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\chi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{y}{x}}}\right).

Recalling the definition of
r
on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
in (
2.14
), we first cut
r
into three parts that will be treated differently:
r=r^{(1)}+r^{(2)}+r^{(3)}
, with

\displaystyle r^{(1)}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{[-1,1]\cap[-2x,2x]}\frac{g(t)-\widetilde{g}(z)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

(B.5)

\displaystyle r^{(2)}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{[-1,1]\setminus[-2x,2x]}\frac{g(t)-\widetilde{g}(z)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

\displaystyle r^{(3)}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{[-1,1]}\dfrac{y^{3}}{6}\frac{\mathrm{i}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)}{t-z}\chi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{y}{x}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}.

We start with
r^{(1)}(z)
.
We write
I=[-1,1]\cap[-2x,2x]
for the domain of integration.
By definition of
\widetilde{g}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{g(t)-\widetilde{g}(z)}{t-z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(t)-g(x)-\mathrm{i}yg^{\prime}(x)+\frac{y^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(t)-g(x)-(t-x)g^{\prime}(x)-\frac{(t-x)^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right)+g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{t-\overline{z}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x),

(B.6)

where we used
((t-x)^{2}+y^{2})/(t-z)=t-\overline{z}
.
Using (
A.16
) together with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert\leq 2x
for the first term on the right-hand side, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im r^{(1)}(z)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{3}x^{p-3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\frac{1}{z(t-z)}}}\right\rvert+x^{p-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\frac{1}{z}}}\right\rvert+x^{p-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\frac{t-\overline{z}}{z}}}\right\rvert}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{Cy}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}\int_{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{3}x^{p-3}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert 2x-t}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert^{2}}+x^{p-1}+x^{p-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert 2x-t}}\right\rvert}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)},

by computing explicitly the imaginary parts. Recalling
I\subset[-2x,2x]
, the last integral is then bounded by
C\int_{I}x^{p-1}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\leq Cx^{p}
.
Using
p\geq 2
and
x\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
yields
\lvert\im r^{(1)}(z)\rvert\leq Cy
.

We now deal with
r^{(2)}(z)
.
We write
J=[-1,1]\setminus[-2x,2x]
for the domain of integration.
Note that this interval is empty if
x>1/2
, so we can further assume that
x\in[0,1/2]
.
By parity,
\int_{J}\frac{g(t)}{t}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=0
so we can write

r^{(2)}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi z}\int_{J}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(t)-\widetilde{g}(z)}{t-z}-\frac{g(t)}{t}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\int_{J}\frac{1}{t-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(t)}{t}-\frac{\widetilde{g}(z)}{z}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}.

(B.7)

Then, using the definition of
\widetilde{g}(z)
and then that
g(t)/t=V^{\prime}(t)
, we decompose

\displaystyle\frac{g(t)}{t}-\frac{\widetilde{g}(z)}{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{g(t)}{t}-\frac{g(x)+\mathrm{i}yg^{\prime}(x)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)}{z}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\prime}(t)-V^{\prime}(x)-\mathrm{i}yV^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\prime}(x)-\frac{g(x)}{z}+\mathrm{i}y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\prime\prime}(x)-\frac{g^{\prime}(x)}{z}}}\right)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}\frac{g^{\prime\prime}(x)}{z}}}\right),

and treat the two terms on the right-hand side differently.
For the first term, note that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V^{\prime}(t)-V^{\prime}(x)-\mathrm{i}yV^{\prime\prime}(x)-(t-z)V^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert V^{\prime}(t)-V^{\prime}(x)-(t-x)V^{\prime\prime}(x)}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{p-3},

using (
A.17
) together with
x\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert
for
t\in J
.
Therefore, the contribution of this first term to
\im r^{(2)}(z)
is

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\int_{J}\frac{V^{\prime}(t)-V^{\prime}(x)-\mathrm{i}yV^{\prime\prime}(x)}{t-z}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\int_{J}V^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}}}\right\rvert+C\int_{J}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\frac{1}{t-z}}}\right\rvert\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 0+Cy\int_{J}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{2}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{p-3}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\leq Cy,

(B.8)

using
p>2
.
For the second term, using that
V^{\prime}(x)-\frac{g(x)}{z}=g(x)(\frac{1}{x}-\frac{1}{z})=pc_{p}x^{p-1}\frac{\mathrm{i}y}{z}
and bounding the imaginary part by the absolute value, we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\int_{J}\frac{1}{t-z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\prime}(x)-\frac{g(x)}{z}+\mathrm{i}y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\prime\prime}(x)-\frac{g^{\prime}(x)}{z}}}\right)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}\frac{g^{\prime\prime}(x)}{z}}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{x^{p-1}y}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}+y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{p-2}+\frac{x^{p-1}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}}}\right)+\frac{y^{2}}{2}\frac{x^{p-2}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}}}\right)\int_{J}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}.

(B.9)

Then, note that

\int_{J}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\leq 2\int_{2x}^{1}\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\leq C\log 1/x,

(B.10)

using here that
x\in[0,1/2]
so that the pole at
x
and the pole at 1 due to
\sigma(t)
are bounded away from each other.
So the right-hand side of (
B.9
) is at most
Cyx^{p-2}\log 1/x\leq Cy
, because
p>2
.
Combining this with (
B.8
) shows
\lvert\im r^{(2)}(z)\rvert\leq Cy
.

Finally, we bound
r^{(3)}(z)
.
Using the formula (
B.3
) to compute explicitly the integral and applying (
2.7
), we get

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r^{(3)}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{Cy^{p\wedge 3}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z^{2}-1}}\right\rvert^{1/2}}\leq Cy^{(p-1)\wedge 2},

(B.11)

where we noted that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z^{2}-1}}\right\rvert^{1/2}\geq cy
by distinguishing whether
z
is closer to
-1
,
0
or
1
.
Since
p\geq 2
, this yields
\lvert\im r^{(3)}(z)\rvert\leq Cy
and concludes the proof of Part
(i)
.

Part
(ii)
.

First recall that
g
is continuous on
\mathbb{C}
(see Remark
2.2
). Continuity of
r
at a point
z_{0}\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
then follows by dominated convergence theorem, using the following domination: for all
t\in[-1,1]
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-z_{0}}}\right\rvert\leq 1
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\frac{g(t)-g(z)}{t-z}}}\right\rvert=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert f_{1,z}(t)}}\right\rvert\leq C,

by Lemma
4.2
.

Continuity at 0 is more subtle due to the
1/z
prefactor.
We reuse the decomposition of the proof of Part
(i)
, but control the pieces in terms of modulus instead of imaginary part. We need less precision in the bounds since we only look for continuity.
Again, by symmetry we can assume
x,y\geq 0
.
Taking back
r^{(1)}(z)
from (
B.5
), and using the same decomposition (
B.6
) as for Part
(i)
, we have for
t\in[-2x,2x]

\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\frac{g(t)-\widetilde{g}(z)}{t-z}}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{3}x^{p-3}+\frac{x^{p-1}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}+\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\overline{z}}}\right\rvert}{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert}x^{p-2}}}\right)\leq Cx^{p-2},

where in the second inequality we used that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert^{3}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-z}}\right\rvert\cdot 3x\cdot 3\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert

for the first term, and

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-\overline{z}}}\right\rvert\leq 2x+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert\leq 3\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert
for the third term. Integrating this bound, we deduce that
r^{(1)}(z)\to 0
as
z\to 0
.
For
r^{(2)}(z)
, we can assume that
x\leq 1/2
, and writing again
J=[-1,1]\setminus[-2x,2x]
, we get from (
B.7
) that

r^{(2)}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{J}\frac{g(t)}{t(t-z)}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}-\frac{\widetilde{g}(z)}{2\pi z}\int_{J}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{(t-z)\sigma(t)}.

Note that the second term vanishes as
z\to 0
by (
B.10
) combined with the fact that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\widetilde{g}(z)/z}}\right\rvert=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert g(x)+iyg^{\prime}(x)-y^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)/2}}\right\rvert/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert\leq c(x^{p-1}+yx^{p-2})\leq Cx^{p-2}
.
For the first term, by dominated convergence theorem, we have

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{J}\frac{g(t)}{t(t-z)}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert>2x}\frac{g(t)}{t(t-z)}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}\xrightarrow[z\to 0]{}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{g(t)}{t^{2}}\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{\sigma(t)}=r(0),

where the domination follows from the following bound, for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert\geq 2x
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\frac{g(t)}{t(t-z)\sigma(t)}}}\right\rvert\leq C\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{p-1}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert\sigma(t)}\leq C\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert^{p-2}}{\sigma(t)},

using that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t-x}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert t}}\right\rvert/2
.
This proves
r^{(1)}(z)\to r(0)
as
z\to 0
.
Finally, the fact that
r^{(3)}(z)\to 0
as
z\to 0
has already been proved in (
B.11
).

Part
(iii)
.

By Lemma
A.3
, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that
\re r=r\geq c_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, by Part
(ii)
, the function
r
is continuous on the compact set
K\cup\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\exists y\in\mathbb{R},x+\mathrm{i}y\in K\}
, and hence uniformly continuous. Combining these two facts yields the result.
∎

The proof of the stability lemma in
[
19
]
relies on two lemmas. The first one
[
19
, Lemma B.2]
contains bounds on the function
b(z)=\sqrt{z+1}\sqrt{z-1}
, which we can directly use in our context.
The second one is
[
19
, Lemma B.3]
and it can be stated as follows in our context, the main differences being that
V^{\prime}(z)
in
[
19
]
is replaced by
g_{\alpha}(z)/z
here.
Note that (
B.15
) and (
B.16
) are not used to prove the stability lemma, but are useful for the proof of the local law.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
p>2
.
There exist
\delta_{0},c,C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta_{0}
and
y\in(0,\delta_{0}]
,

\displaystyle c\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C

(B.12)

\displaystyle\im(r_{\alpha}(z)b(z))\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{z}}}\right)}}\right\rvert

(B.13)

Moreover, if
x\in[-1-y,1+y]
,

\im(r_{\alpha}(z)b(z))\geq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)}}\right\rvert

(B.14)

and, if
x\notin[-1-y,1+y]
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im s_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{Cy}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert b(z)}}\right\rvert}

(B.15)

\displaystyle\re(s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{0})-\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z))\geq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)}}\right\rvert

(B.16)

where
z_{0}\coloneqq 1+y
if
x>1+y
, and
z_{0}\coloneqq-1-y
if
x<-1-y
.

Proof.

We highlight below the modifications needed in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma B.3]
.

Firstly, by Lemma
B.1
, there exist
\delta_{0},c,C>0
such that, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert\leq 1+\delta_{0}
and
y\in(0,\delta_{0}]
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im r_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq Cy,\qquad\re r_{\alpha}(z)\geq c,\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)}}\right\rvert\leq C.

(B.17)

In particular, this implies (
B.12
).
Secondly, we prove that there exists
C>0
such that for any
z=x+iy
with
y>0
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{z}}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq Cy.

(B.18)

In view of
g_{\alpha}(z)=\alpha g(z)+4(1-\alpha)z^{2}
, it suffices to establish (
B.18
) for
\alpha=1
.
Recalling the definition of
g(z)
in (
2.2
) and bounding the last term in
g(z)
as in (
2.7
), we find

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\im\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g(z)}{z}}}\right)}}\right\rvert=\frac{1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x\im g(z)-y\re g(z)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z}}\right\rvert^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert y^{p\wedge 3}+y\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p}+y^{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert x}}\right\rvert^{p-2}}}\right)\leq Cy,

using
p\geq 2
and that
x
and
y
are in a compact set, proving (
B.18
).
The inequalities (
B.17
) and (
B.18
) are exactly the inputs which are used in
[
19
]
to prove (
B.13
), (
B.14
) and (
B.15
), so the rest of the proof is identical (recall that our function
b
is the same as theirs).

Lastly, the proof of (
B.16
) requires slight modifications compared to the one of
[
19
, Eq. (B.11)]
.
As argued there, if
x\notin[-1-y,1+y]
, we have

\re(r_{\alpha}(z)b(z))\geq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)}}\right\rvert
, and therefore, by (
2.15
) and (
2.16
),

\displaystyle\re(s_{V_{\alpha}}(z_{0})-\widetilde{s}_{V_{\alpha}}(z))

\displaystyle=\re\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{2z}-\frac{g_{\alpha}(z_{0})}{2z_{0}}+r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)+r_{\alpha}(z_{0})b(z_{0})}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq c\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)}}\right\rvert-\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{z}-\frac{g_{\alpha}(z_{0})}{z_{0}}}}\right\rvert.

(B.19)

Since
z\mapsto g_{\alpha}(z)/z
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
(as a function of two variables, not as a function of a complex variable), we have, for
z
in the region considered here,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert\frac{g_{\alpha}(z)}{z}-\frac{g_{\alpha}(z_{0})}{z_{0}}}}\right\rvert\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-z_{0}}}\right\rvert\leq C\kappa,

where
\kappa=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z+1}}\right\rvert\vee\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert z-1}}\right\rvert
.
On the other hand, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lvert r_{\alpha}(z)b(z)}}\right\rvert\geq c\sqrt{\kappa}
near
\pm 1
. Therefore, one can choose
\delta_{0}
small enough so that, on the right-hand side of (
B.19
), the first term is at least twice larger as the second one, from which we conclude on (
B.16
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.6
.

The proof is identical to the one of
[
19
, Lemma B.1]
, with
[
19
, Lemma B.3]
replaced by Lemma
B.2
here.
∎
